§2.6 Implicit Differentiation · Exam Map
Midterm 2: about 6 points (5–8) · ★★

[Proof 10] Power Rule for rational exponents (Exercise 50)

Implicitly defined function (p. 165)
f is defined implicitly by x³ + y³ = 6xy:
x³ + [f(x)]³ = 6x·f(x) for all x in dom f ^GYzWdsjC

One relation, several functions (p. 165)
x² + y² = 25 ⇒ y = ±√(25 − x²):
f = upper, g = lower semicircle
folium x³ + y³ = 6xy: three functions ^Ul0OU80X

Implicit differentiation (p. 165)
differentiate both sides w.r.t. x,
then solve the result for dy/dx
Assumed: y is a differentiable function of x ^IGPiipXr

Midterm 1 Q5, Q7
points lost for a missing condition
(h ≠ 0; a third δ) ^uSji2s4x

Chain Rule (§2.5)
d/dx g(u) = g′(u)·du/dx ^iIhHiKEC

Product and Quotient Rules (§2.3)
(fg)′ = f′g + fg′
(f/g)′ = (f′g − fg′)/g² ^9nlwwt1D

Chain Rule on a y-term (p. 166)
d/dx(y²) = 2y·dy/dx,  d/dx(y³) = 3y²·y′
d/dx(6xy) = 6xy′ + 6y  (p. 167)
notation: y′ = dy/dx ^jREbeI2K

Note 1 (pp. 166–167)
y′ = −x/y is right for every function
the equation defines:
−x/√(25 − x²) on f,  x/√(25 − x²) on g ^c6yYjnY1

Why implicit (pp. 166–168)
• easier even when y can be solved (Ex. 1)
• Note 2: the cubic formula gives 3 huge formulas
• y⁵ + 3x²y² + 5x⁴ = 12: y cannot be solved
• Abel, Galois: no radical formula, degree ≥ 5 ^H8oCMXXL

1  assume y is a differentiable
    function of x ^fKsF3L7m

2  d/dx both sides: every y-term gets ·y′,
    xy-terms use the Product Rule ^RBQb2sTK

3  collect the y′ terms, factor out y′ ^ZCSKk4EK

4  y′ = N/D   (D ≠ 0)
5  evaluate only at points ON the curve ^WFSLPbYB

Points
• write: assume y is differentiable
• the factor y′ on every y-term
• (denominator ≠ 0) before dividing
• the point must lie on the curve ^4CKRJqfD
